| D3.91 | `GroupCommitWriter` — **the group-commit cursor lives in MEMORY, so after a restart every recovered row looks uncommitted** | **opened ARC 028 (S2) by the author in the same motion as the writer, open.** §9 is *"enqueue → durable local WAL → shared-pool writer → group-commit to Postgres. Crash gap healed by startup reconciliation vs broker truth."* The writer commits only from the WAL's DURABLE PREFIX, which makes the crash gap one-sided by construction — Postgres can never hold a row the WAL lost — but the cursor recording *how far the sink got* is `self._cursor`, an int on the object. A restart zeroes it and the whole WAL re-presents. **Deliberate, and the alternative was rejected rather than overlooked:** a persisted cursor is a second durable artifact whose own fsync discipline nothing in this arc proves, and a cursor that is behind is safe (duplicate rows, caught by the append-only log's own keys) while a cursor that is AHEAD silently drops rows. Choosing the safe failure and naming it beats inventing a durability nobody measured. Discharge with §9's cold-start reconciliation, which is explicitly not in this arc | — | limiter |
| D3.92 | `CommitSinkPort` has **no Postgres implementation, and the only sink in the tree is `RecordingSink` (a list)** | **opened ARC 028 (S2), open, and stated in the gate's verdict rather than implied away.** `checks/check_plane1_wal.py` ARM 5 drives §12.4's degraded-sink branch against `RecordingSink` with `fail_with` set, which is a real object raising a real exception through the real `drain_once` — but it is not a transaction, not a connection pool, and not `nix_db_schema_spec.docx`'s `trade_history`. **Nothing measured here says a Postgres group-commit works**, and the gate's docstring says so in the same words. What IS measured: the seam's shape, the buffering, the alert, the backlog and the drain. Discharge is full Postgres schema integration, which the arc brief put out of scope | — | limiter |
| D3.93 | **NO INSTRUMENT IN THIS TREE OBSERVES A POWER CUT, and the fsync arm is the whole of the durability claim** | **opened ARC 028 (S2), open, and it is the honest half of a green.** `check_plane1_wal` ARM 1 observes the `fsync(2)` SYSCALL under `strace -y`, annotated with the WAL's own path — that is a real observation of a real syscall against a real file. What it does NOT observe is whether the DEVICE honoured it: a filesystem mounted `nobarrier`, a disk with a lying write cache, or a virtualised block layer can all return success from `fsync` having persisted nothing, and no software instrument on the machine can tell. ARM 2's SIGKILL cannot substitute — **MEASURED in the drill's own output: after the kill, 4128 rows were readable and only 32 were inside the last fsync**, because a killed process's dirty pages belong to a kernel that is still running. The gap is printed in the gate's evidence precisely so a green cannot be read as power-loss durability. Discharge needs hardware (a real power interruption, or a device-level barrier test), not a better test | — | limiter |
| D3.94 | `check_picture_atomicity` — **the measured arm CANNOT tear by construction, so the plants prove the DETECTOR's power and not the harness's coverage of every interleaving** | **opened ARC 028 (S2) by the gate's author, open.** `FinancialPictureBook.current()` is one `LOAD_ATTR` over an immutable snapshot; there is no window between two stores for a reader to land in, so the measured arm's zero tears is a structural consequence rather than a race survived. That is the right implementation and the wrong thing to claim power over. **What the gate does instead, and reports as a number:** it runs two plants under the same writer, the same thread structure and the same hostile switch interval, and prints the WEAKER plant's tear rate beside the measured arm's read count — measured on this node at 2.2–5% for the two-attribute publisher and 99.7% for the two-read consumer, against 0 tears in 2000 reads. So the statement is *a defect of this shape would have been caught at this rate*, not *every interleaving was explored*. Discharge needs a model checker or a deterministic-interleaving harness; a longer race buys nothing against a window of zero | — | limiter |
| D3.95 | `picture.picture_defects` — **the production detector is WEAKER than the gate's, and cannot see the tear §3 actually names** | **opened ARC 028 (S2), open, and asserted rather than described: `test_picture_defects_CANNOT_see_the_coherent_STALE_BALANCE_tear` fails the day it becomes untrue.** §3's hazard is *"headroom off a stale balance + fresh commitment"*. A publisher that reads balance at generation *k*, the table at *k+1*, and derives Σ open margin / committed / deployable from what it read emits a snapshot whose every derived field follows perfectly from the inputs beside it — and `balance` is an independent input nothing else in the snapshot constrains. One snapshot does not carry the information. The gate closes this with a GENERATION LINK planted into the writer's world (balance and every row's margin carry the same generation), which is available to an instrument and not to the Limiter. Discharge would mean putting something in the published picture that ties balance to the table — a balance-version field, or a hash of the inputs — which is a change to the FROZEN seam and belongs to an arc with the authority to amend it (cf. D3.41) | — | limiter |
| D3.96 | `wal_kill_drill` torn-tail arm — **the CUT is staged, only the DEATH is raced** | **opened ARC 028 (S2) by the author, open, and recorded because the alternative reads stronger than it is.** The arm kills a real child (reaped `-9`) that has written the first half of a record's bytes and then blocked, so `recover()` meets a genuinely partial record left by a genuinely dead process. What is NOT raced is the position of the cut: a kill landing inside a small `write(2)` to a regular file is unwinnable, because the kernel completes the write whatever signal is pending. So the arm proves *the reader tolerates the shape a crash mid-flush leaves* and not *a crash produced this shape unaided*. Discharge needs a large buffered flush interrupted mid-syscall, or a device-level fault injector (`dm-flakey`), both of which are heavier than the property is worth today | — | limiter |
| D3.97 | `check_plane1_wal` — **the durability property degrades to CANNOT_MEASURE on any host without `strace` or with `ptrace_scope=2`, and `ptrace` is not in the observer's vocabulary** | **opened ARC 028 (S2), open.** Two halves. (1) ARM 1 is the ONLY instrument that observes the fsync, and it needs `/usr/bin/strace` plus permission to trace a descendant. This node has strace and `kernel.yama.ptrace_scope=1` (descendants tracable), which is why it works; on a hardened host (`ptrace_scope=2`, or no strace) the gate correctly returns CANNOT_MEASURE per §17 rather than PASS — but the practical effect is that Plane-1 durability is UNPROVEN there, silently, from the operator's point of view. (2) `RESOURCES` declares `ptrace`, which `nixverify.observe.covers()` matches by exact string only and which no audit event can ever produce (the syscall is made by the strace subprocess, not by this interpreter), so the claim is PLANNER-ONLY — declared because it is true, not because the observer can confirm it, exactly as `check_feed_kill_drill` declares `cpu-affinity`. Discharge with either a second, tracer-free fsync observer or a documented host precondition | — | verify |
| D3.98 | `Plane1Wal` opens the WAL `buffering=0`, so **every `enqueue` is a `write(2)` on the HOT PATH and nothing bounds its cost** | **opened ARC 028 (S2) by the author, open, and it is a real tension rather than a nit.** §11 makes the entry pathway *"cache reads + arithmetic only"* and §11.6 puts group-commit off the hot path. Unbuffered was chosen deliberately: with a userspace buffer, a SIGKILL takes whatever had not been flushed and the loss is invisible from outside, which would make the crash gap unmeasurable — the very property `check_plane1_wal` ARM 2 exists to measure. The cost is a syscall per row on the path §11 wants to be arithmetic. **No instrument bounds it**, and D3.46's objection applies exactly: a wall-clock figure would be a function of machine load and is therefore not a verdict input. Discharge needs a load-independent cost instrument (a syscall count per enqueue, which is falsifiable and cheap) plus a decision about whether a small buffer flushed per event is the better trade | — | limiter |
| D3.99 | **Two artifacts appeared in the CANONICAL TREE during this arc that NO COMMIT ON ANY BRANCH CONTAINS, and no sub-agent reported them** | ARC 028 (Stage 3 convergence), MEASURED, and found by `pre-commit` reddening on a file the integrator had never seen | unassigned — **needs an operator ruling on provenance before the work is adopted, not a fix.** `scripts/nix_status.sh` (561 lines) and `scripts/tests/test_nix_status.py` (250 lines) were on disk at `/home/bbt/nix`, staged by the mandated `git add -A`, and broke three commit gates (`ruff format`, `pylint` R1721, `bandit` B607) — which is the only reason they were noticed at all. **Measured provenance, four ways, all negative:** `git log --all -- <both paths>` returns nothing; `git cat-file -e HEAD:<path>` says *exists on disk, but not in HEAD*; `git grep -l nix_status HEAD` returns nothing, so nothing COMMITTED references either; and a working-tree grep returns only the two files themselves — a closed, self-referential pair. They did not exist at arc start (the opening `git status --short` showed exactly one untracked path, the arc brief). Timestamps 2026-08-12 17:26:52 and 17:27:27, inside the Stage 1 window. **The test's own docstring claims `nix_status.sh` v1.0.0 'shipped' with two faults and that the dashboard rendered 2 of 28 checks — but v1.0.0 is in no commit either, so the defect it reports is a defect in an artifact that never shipped.** Most plausible cause: a sub-agent wrote into the canonical tree despite an explicit instruction to work only inside its provisioned worktree, then committed on its own branch and left these behind; all five were told, and none reported this work. **DISPOSITION AMENDED 2026-08-12, SAME DAY, BY THE OPERATOR: `scripts/nix_status.sh` IS THE OPERATOR'S OWN WORK.** They asked for two changes to it by name, which resolves the provenance question the row was opened for. Both files were restored from the preserved copies and verified byte-identical against the recorded sha256 before any edit. **The row stays OPEN, and its subject is now the second half, which the operator's answer does not touch: worktree isolation is REQUESTED, NOT ENFORCED.** Something wrote into the canonical tree while five sub-agents were told to stay inside provisioned worktrees, and the write was caught only because `git add -A` staged it into three commit gates — the standing rule getting lucky, not a detector. Note also that the file as delivered **never passed this tree's own gates**: `pylint` R1721 and `bandit` B607 both fire on lines that predate ARC 029's edits, which is consistent with work that never went through a commit. Original disposition, superseded and kept for the record: unattributable work is not merged into a safety spine on the strength of a plausible docstring. Nothing durable was lost — the pair was in no commit — and both files are preserved with their sha256 (`9cb86ec4…`, `f999395f…`) so an operator can restore them deliberately. **This is ARC 024's failure class inverted and it is why `CLAUDE.md`'s write-back gate exists:** ARC 024 passed its close-out with 30 paths staged and never committed, and an mtime proved they were written while a `git checkout` would have erased them. Here the same state produced work nobody could attribute. The general rule: **a file's presence on disk is evidence of nothing; `HEAD`'s tree is the record** | verify |
| D3.100 | **D3.40's class RECURRED INSIDE THE MECHANISM BUILT TO PREVENT IT — §0g's own control could not survive the close-out that arms it** | ARC 028 (close-out), MEASURED at the write-back, and found by the control REDDENING rather than by reading | unassigned — **the control is repaired and bound; this row exists because the RECURRENCE is the finding, not the repair.** D3.40 recorded that a guard whose owner is derived from the close-out record dies at the close-out. ARC 028 (C) built §0g to prevent it: `in_flight_arc` derives the running arc as the newest CHECK-DEBT series row that `sessions/SESSION.md` does not close, and `guard_owner_assignment_defect` refuses that arc as an owner AT WRITE TIME. The rule is correct. **Its control asserted `in_flight_arc(REPO) is not None` — and the close-out APPENDS the very `##` heading that closes the newest series row, so the assertion is FALSE BY CONSTRUCTION, for every arc, forever.** It went red at the write-back of the arc that built it, exactly as D3.40 went red at the write-back of the arc that owned it, one layer down. The tense asymmetry `in_flight_arc`'s own docstring documents is the mechanism in both cases. **Repair, and it is stricter than what it replaced:** two arms that cannot satisfy each other — the rule is now driven against a CONSTRUCTED in-flight arc, so the §0g arm is exercised on every run in every phase of every arc rather than only mid-arc, which is the half of the cycle nobody reads; and the real records are still read, with `in_flight_arc` required to ANSWER (an error is a failure, because *which arc is running* being unanswerable is the reading that turns §0g off silently) while an answer of *none* — the between-arcs and close-out state — is reported rather than asserted away. Re-bound by a plant that leaves the helper in place and neuters only the rule, so it fails on the PROPERTY: `assert 'is the arc IN FLIGHT' in ''`. **The general rule, now stated twice from two directions: any instrument whose input is derived from the close-out record must be exercised against a CONSTRUCTED state, not only against the live one** — the live one is guaranteed to be the wrong state at exactly the moment the instrument is read | verify |

| D3.101 | **The tree's own venv interpreter is INVISIBLE to all four census predicates when it is on `PATH`, unactivated, and invoked by bare name** | ARC 029 (0.1), MEASURED, opened by the author of the control repair in the same motion as the repair | **ARC 030 (architect ruling, ARC 029 Phase 0, item 3)** — recorded-not-repaired is ratified, because no `/proc` fact distinguishes the case and a repair would be a guess wearing a predicate; the row carries a NAMED owner rather than `unassigned`, since an unowned row is the §0f/§0g failure the binding table exists to prevent. **the honest closure is D1.42's, not a fifth predicate.** Phase 0.1 measured `nix_processes`' union against the tree's OWN interpreter in three spellings, and the third is blind: `absolute path, unactivated` → `_mentions_home=True`, seen; `activated, bare python` → `_runs_tree_venv=True`, seen; **`PATH=…/.venv/bin`, `VIRTUAL_ENV` unset, argv[0]=`python`** → every predicate False, **not seen**. `nix_processes`' docstring already concedes a residual miss, but it describes it as *"a Nix process running a SYSTEM interpreter, outside the slice, with no path argument and no `VIRTUAL_ENV`"* — and this process is running **the tree's own venv interpreter**, which the concession's wording excludes. The concession is therefore NARROWER THAN THE HOLE, which is the D3.19 class (nothing is discharged by being named, and least of all by being named inaccurately). **Why it is recorded rather than repaired:** no `/proc` fact distinguishes this case. `/proc/<pid>/exe` resolves the venv symlink to `/usr/bin/python3.14`, so attributing on the image alone would sweep every unrelated system-Python process on the node into a §10 core census — the exact over-attribution `test_VIRTUAL_ENV_ALONE_does_NOT_make_a_SHELL_a_nix_process` exists to prevent; argv carries no path by construction; `VIRTUAL_ENV` is absent by construction. The venv's identity survives in `sys.prefix`, which is derived INSIDE the process from `pyvenv.cfg` beside the resolved argv[0] and is not exported to any kernel interface. **Direction of the error: under-attribution — the dangerous half for a census whose failure mode is missing a process that occupies a reserved core** (the opposite direction from D3.84, and the two now bracket the union from both sides). Closing it properly means **joining the units to `nix-trading.slice`** (D1.42), after which the kernel's own answer is authoritative and every heuristic becomes belt-and-braces | verify |
| D3.102 | **The ARC 028 subset numerator "twenty-nine" is UNDERIVED — its denominator was corrected without re-deriving it** | ARC 029 (0.2), opened by the arc that corrected the denominator, in the same motion, rather than left for a reader to find | **ARC 030 (architect ruling, ARC 029 Phase 0, item 2)** — **do NOT audit the forty-one rows now**; that is a scope expansion inside the blocking phase of an arc with four Phase 0 items, four sub-agents and three stages still ahead. The ARC 028 series row narrated *"Twenty-nine of the thirty-six new rows were opened by an instrument"*. ARC 029 / 0.2 proved the DENOMINATOR wrong from the row's own enumeration — 41 opened, not 36 — and corrected it. **The numerator was not re-derived and is not claimed to be right.** Establishing whether twenty-nine is still the correct count of instrument-opened rows means auditing the provenance of all forty-one, which is a measurement neither ARC 028 nor ARC 029 took. It is marked `[UNDERIVED — see D3.102]` AT THE SITE rather than restated, silently dropped, or replaced with a guess: **an underived figure left visible is the honest state**, and a corrected sentence that reads as though both halves were checked is precisely the class this arc built the instrument for. Discharge = derive the numerator by attributing each of the forty-one rows to an instrument, a failed plant, or an author, and reconcile it against the sentence. Note for the discharging arc: `restated_figures.py`'s `_STATED_OF_THE` reconciles the DENOMINATOR of an `N of the M new rows` claim and deliberately never the numerator, so no instrument in this tree is watching this figure — which is why it needs a row rather than a gate | verify |
| D3.103 | **`check_untracked_attribution` cannot see an escape into an IGNORED path, which is failure mode #14 at the canonical tree** | ARC 029 (0.3), opened by the author of the gate in the same motion as the gate, and stated in the gate's own docstring rather than discovered later | **ARC 030** — the gate enumerates `git status --porcelain -uall`, which is exactly the population `git add -A` would stage and exactly the population that escaped in ARC 028. **It is blind to everything `.gitignore` excludes**: `state/` wholesale (already D1.16's subject — `state/encrypt_credentials.py` is real Python no hook has ever read), `.venv/`, every `__pycache__`, and any path a future ignore rule adds. A sub-agent writing into an ignored directory is unseen here for precisely the reason it is unseen by `pre-commit run --all-files`, and the two blindnesses share a root: **a scope set by git's tracking state**, this project's most-repeated defect class (seven instances by ARC 026's count). Adding `--ignored` was considered and REFUSED as the wrong trade: it would return thousands of cache entries per run and drown the signal, turning a loud gate into one nobody reads. The other two rows (`gitenv.py`, `registry.py`) and `measurement_path.py` are BELOW the ceiling and remain legitimately owned. **The ruling needed is one of three:** (a) discharge the thirteen by giving them real per-artifact coverage, which is a bulk retrofit of the size ARC 025 attempted; (b) grant a fourth owner explicitly, which sets the precedent the ceiling exists to refuse; or (c) rule that these thirteen are permanently accepted and move them out of a guard into a declared exclusion with its own justification. **Phase 4 baseline moves: 1 FAIL becomes 2.** ARC 029 did not create the debt; it made four arcs of deferral legible by removing the stale owner that was hiding it. **ARCHITECT RULING (recorded ARC 029): OPTION (c), as a HOLDING state for ARC 029 ONLY, not the final disposition.** The thirteen ceiling-tripped artifacts are moved out of the guard into a declared, TEMPORARY `exclusions` bucket in `gate_coverage_baseline.json`, each with a written justification, all owned by `ARC 030`, and the mechanism is recorded as **CHECK-A8**: an exclusion escapes the re-owning ceiling and NOTHING else — still inside the one-way ratchet, still owned by a live arc (a completed owner is CANNOT_MEASURE), still assigned under §0g, and required to justify itself and declare itself temporary. **ARC 030 is committed as the bulk-retrofit arc:** it builds real per-artifact can-fail coverage for all thirteen, empties the exclusion, and drives `check_artifact_gate_coverage` green BY MEASUREMENT. The three below-ceiling artifacts (`gitenv.py`, `measurement_path.py`, `registry.py`) are untouched | verify |

| D3.118 | **`nixverify.observe`'s `os.remove`/`os.rename`/`os.mkdir`/`os.rmdir`/`os.truncate` event handling does not resolve `dir_fd`-relative targets, so ANY check using `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` cleanup reads as a false RESOURCES declaration no honest, non-literal declaration can cover** | ARC 030 (Stage 2, sub-agent B), MEASURED directly: a minimal `sys.addaudithook` reproduction isolating `os.remove` events during `checks/check_runtime_gate.py`'s tempdir cleanup recorded `('os.remove', ('sample.py', 4))` — a bare basename plus a raw `dir_fd` integer, never the absolute directory the deletion actually targets | unassigned — `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory.__exit__` (via `shutil.rmtree`'s fd-relative deletion path, the TOCTOU-safe strategy CPython uses when the platform supports `os.open(..., dir_fd=...)`) unlinks each entry with `os.unlink(entry.name, dir_fd=parent_fd)`, and the `open`/`os.remove` PEP 578 audit event fires with that literal `(name, dir_fd)` pair — `nixverify.observe._on_open`/`_on_path` call `os.fsdecode(args[0])` and never resolve `dir_fd` back to a directory, so the recorded claim is a bare basename (`file-write:sample.py`) with no path segment any `file-write:<root>` declaration's `_under()` prefix check can match. **Measured as a false positive, not a false declaration**: `checks/check_broker_order_config.py` and `checks/check_runtime_gate.py` both declare `file-write:/tmp` (the directory their tempfiles genuinely live under, verified: every `open()`-sourced write claim for both is covered) and `check_observed_resource_claims` still names 2 and 8 residual claims respectively, one per temp filename, purely from `TemporaryDirectory` teardown. A literal per-filename token (`file-write:sample.py`) would clear the FAIL but is exactly the anchor doctrine C.4 forbids — the names are test fixture noise, not a resource claim with any operational meaning, and a future renamed fixture would need the declaration edited in lockstep for a defect the property has nothing to do with. **This check was already FAILING on the pristine pre-ARC-030 tree** (3 unrelated stale `subprocess:python3`-vs-`subprocess:.venv/bin/python` declarations from the CRUCIBLE-DEPSPLIT venv rename, `check_derived_claims`/`check_hook_suite`/`check_order_path_bans` — untouched, not this arc's scope), so this row adds two more names to an existing red rather than causing a new one. Discharge needs `nixverify.observe` to resolve a `dir_fd` argument via `/proc/self/fd/<n>` (Linux-specific, matches this project's Ubuntu-only scope) back to its directory before decoding, so the recorded claim carries the real absolute path an ordinary `file-write:<root>` declaration already covers | verify |

| D3.114 | `check_untracked_attribution`'s new foreign-commit arm (ARC 030 / Stage 2 A3) cannot attribute a commit made on a DETACHED HEAD in a worktree that is later removed without ever being named by a surviving branch or tag | ARC 030 (Stage 2 A), MEASURED directly, twice: once by hand against a scratch 5-worktree repo (`git log --all` finds the detached commit while its worktree lives, `git fsck --unreachable` confirms the object still exists after `git worktree remove --force`, `git log --all` then finds NOTHING), and again as a committed, re-runnable proof — `scripts/tests/test_check_untracked_attribution.py::test_a_DETACHED_worktree_commit_becomes_INVISIBLE_the_instant_its_worktree_is_removed` | unassigned — no git fact available AT GATE TIME recovers this. `git branch --contains <sha>` answers "which branches hold a SHA I already have" (the wrong direction — this needs "does anything hold a SHA I don't know to ask about"); `git fsck --unreachable` finds the dangling object but returns a bare SHA with no path/branch/author to attribute it BY, and is unbounded work over the whole object store on every gate run, not a targeted query. Closing this needs one of: (a) a policy that every worktree this project dispatches is created `-b <branch>` (never `--detach`), so a torn-down worktree's commits stay attributable via the surviving branch ref indefinitely — a PROCESS control, not a git-fact one, and not something a gate can verify was followed after the fact; or (b) recording a per-worktree manifest at creation time (sha + purpose) that a gate could cross-check against `git fsck --unreachable`'s output before the object is gc'd — new tracked state this arc did not build. Named per the mandate rather than built around: "an honest limit beats a green that measured nothing" | verify |

| D3.119 | **[renumbered from D3.117 at Stage 3 integration — sub-agent A and sub-agent B independently picked the same next-free number from their separate worktrees, a real collision this integration step is required to catch; see the series row below] `git stash` is NOT per-worktree — `refs/stash` is ONE ref in the shared common `.git` dir, and two worktrees' concurrent `git stash push` races it** — the worst-case outcome is one worktree's stash silently overwriting/shadowing another's on the shared ref | ARC 030 (Stage 2 A1), MEASURED LIVE, not simulated: this sub-agent ran a routine `git stash` (to diff its own working tree against a clean baseline) while sub-agent B concurrently ran `git stash -u` in `nix-wt-stage2-b` — `git stash list` immediately afterward showed exactly ONE entry, `WIP on arc-030-stage2-b`, and this sub-agent's own tracked-file changes (six modified files, ~600 lines) were NOT on the stash list at all. `git fsck --no-reflog --unreachable` recovered the exact commit (`540c2946a91640387fc9c17f72548c19cc186902`, matched by content and timestamp against three near-identical dangling `WIP on arc-030-stage2-a` candidates) and `git stash apply <sha>` restored it byte-for-byte (`git status --short` after apply listed all ten expected modified files, confirmed against the pre-recovery edit history). **Nothing was lost, but only because the object store itself is safe** (content-addressed, unaffected by the ref race) **and `git fsck --unreachable` could still find the orphaned commit before gc** — a real workflow that trusted `git stash pop`'s exit code alone, without independently verifying the restored file set, would have silently discarded real work exactly as ARC 029 did, one layer lower (a git REF race rather than a shared INDEX). This is the single most concrete, unplanned confirmation this arc produced that Stage 2's premise is correct: worktree/index isolation genuinely does NOT cover every shared git surface | unassigned — `git stash` is ordinary developer/agent tooling, not something `verify.py` gates today, and no dispatched agent in this project's harness is instructed to use `git stash` as a matter of course (this sub-agent's use was ad hoc, for its own baseline comparison, not part of any arc's mandated workflow). Discharge = either a standing instruction that dispatched agents never use `git stash` (use a scratch branch/commit instead, which IS per-worktree) recorded in CLAUDE.md or a rules file, or — if `git stash` use persists — a check that verifies no `refs/stash` entry has been silently superseded mid-arc (hard to build: the very nature of the race is that the superseded entry leaves no trace on the ref itself, only in the object store, which is not indexed for this purpose) | verify |

| D3.121 | **The published financial picture carries NO per-key venue timestamp/sequence, so §6.4b's MONOTONIC-BY-SOURCE guard cannot bind on the real wire — the Allocator's mirror degrades to a whole-snapshot recency guard and COUNTS the degradation** | ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent A), MEASURED while building `scripts/nixalloc/mirror.py` — `nixalloc.seam.MIRRORED_FIELDS` pins nine fields at `SEAM_REV 1.0.0` and not one of them is a venue stamp; `published_ts` is the LIMITER's own clock (`nixrisk.picture.FinancialPictureBook._build` stamps it with its injected `clock()`), and `nixrisk.picture.encode_picture` emits exactly those nine keys, so nothing on the wire carries what §6.4b requires | **ARC 032** — §6.4b: *"every reading from the venue — balance, per-symbol margin, and position/quantity updates ... carries the venue's own timestamp/sequence number"*, and the guard is *"per key ... so a late update on one key can never regress another"*. The rule is expressible; the WIRE is not. `AllocatorMirror` therefore takes the stamps as a field riding BESIDE the picture (`MirrorUpdate.source_stamps`, wire key `source_stamps`, keyed `balance` / `margin:<symbol>` / `position:<trade_id>`), which `check_allocator_mirror`'s transport arm publishes over a real `ipc://` socket and proves the guard binds against — but the LIMITER does not emit that field today, so against the real publisher every key is stamped with the picture's own `published_ts` and the per-key guard degenerates to a whole-snapshot recency guard. **That is strictly stronger than nothing and strictly weaker than §6.4b, and it is COUNTED (`AllocatorMirror.degraded_source_stamps`) rather than logged as prose**, because a guard that quietly weakens itself is the defect §6.4b exists to prevent one layer up; `test_A3_an_unstamped_reading_falls_back_to_published_ts_and_COUNTS_it` asserts the counter. Discharge needs the publisher side: either `SEAM_REV` bumped with per-key stamps folded into `FinancialPicture` (a frozen-seam change, so an architect ruling), or `encode_picture` emitting `source_stamps` alongside as this consumer already reads. **Not fixable from the Allocator's side at all** — merging a fresher key into a held picture would make the Allocator a second maintainer of the financial picture (§2), which is the authority violation this arc exists not to introduce | verify |
| D3.122 | `check_allocator_mirror`'s transport arm proves `ipc://` delivery and snapshot-on-subscribe **between two sockets in ONE process**, never across a real process boundary | ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent A), recorded at construction — the gate binds a real AF_UNIX endpoint (the socket file's existence on disk is asserted, so the kernel really did materialise it and the bytes really do traverse it) but spawns nothing | **ARC 032** — the same limit `check_state_bus` records for itself, inherited here because this gate reuses that transport. fd inheritance, filesystem permissions on the socket, and SELinux/AppArmor labelling are all UNPROVEN, and §12.7's actual topology is Limiter process -> Allocator process on separate cores (§10). Discharge = a two-process drill (the shape `check_plane2_across_kill` already uses) in which the publisher is a real child and the Allocator's mirror reaches FRESH across the boundary. Named rather than papered over: an honest limit beats a green that measured nothing | verify |
| D3.123 | **The tree now holds TWO consumer-side mirrors of §3's ONE table, with different staleness vocabularies and different guards, and neither gate judges the other** | ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent A), MEASURED by reading both: `nixrisk.picture.PictureMirror` (ARC 028) answers `tradable() -> (bool, str)` and has no state enumeration and no §6.4b guard; `nixalloc.mirror.AllocatorMirror` (this arc) implements the frozen `nixalloc.seam.MirrorPort`, carries `MirrorState` EMPTY/PARTIAL/FRESH/STALE and the per-key monotonic guard | **ARC 032** — they are not duplicates by accident: `PictureMirror` is the LIMITER package's own consumer-side reader and predates the Allocator seam, and the two answer the same question (*may this process size on what it holds?*) with two different predicates. Today nothing is broken — no production caller reads both — but §12.7 has ONE mirror model, and two implementations of it will drift the moment one gains a rule the other does not. Discharge = an architect ruling on which survives, then either retire `PictureMirror` in favour of `AllocatorMirror` (which subsumes it: same codec, same freshness ceiling, plus the state enumeration and the §6.4b guard) or state in both docstrings why two are correct. Deliberately NOT done unilaterally this arc: `scripts/nixrisk/picture.py` is another agent's subject and `checks/check_picture_atomicity.py` is bound to it | verify |
| D3.124 | **A tree-scoped gate that imports its subject BY NAME silently falls through to the LIVE repository, and two shipped gates PASS over an empty directory today** | ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent A), MEASURED, self-found and then reproduced on siblings. Self: the first draft of `check_allocator_mirror` returned **PASS** when pointed at an empty `tmp_path`, caught by its own can-fail suite (`test_a_MISSING_subject_is_cannot_measure_never_a_PASS`). Siblings, each re-run in a FRESH interpreter against a fresh empty directory: `check_coldstart` -> **pass**, `check_survival_watch` -> **pass**, while `check_flatten`, `check_limiter_gate`, `check_reservation_lifecycle` and `check_allocator_seam` correctly returned `cannot_measure` | **ARC 032** — the mechanism is `checks/_preamble.py`, which appends the REAL `scripts/` to `sys.path` permanently and deliberately never removes it (`nixverify/loader.py` records why). A gate's `load()` inserts `<nix_home>/scripts` at `sys.path[0]`; when that directory does not contain the subject the import does not fail, it RESOLVES AGAINST THE LIVE TREE, and every arm then drives the pristine repository while the verdict is attributed to the tree under judgement. This is doctrine C.4's moving anchor in its most literal form and it is worse than a false negative: it is a gate reporting on a subject it never read. **Closed in `check_allocator_mirror` by `_provenance_defect`**, which reads each loaded module's `__file__` — the interpreter's own record of where the bytes came from — and returns CANNOT_MEASURE naming the foreign path (the can-fail suite asserts the `is NOT under` reason, so the closure is itself gated). Discharge = the same three-line provenance assertion in every `load()`-style gate, plus a can-fail case per gate proving an empty tree is CANNOT_MEASURE. **The two named PASSes are live today**; they were not repaired here because `checks/check_coldstart.py` and `checks/check_survival_watch.py` are outside this sub-agent's file ownership and a cross-agent edit mid-arc is the collision D3.119 was opened for | verify |
| D3.125 | `AllocatorMirror`'s freshness ceiling (`max_age_s`) has no §12A tunable and no `risks/*.json` entry, so every caller picks its own | ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent A), recorded at construction — grepped `risks/` and §12A for a mirror-staleness knob and found none; the class therefore REFUSES a default (`MirrorUsageError`, asserted by `test_A2_the_freshness_ceiling_has_no_silent_default`), following `FinancialPictureBook`'s refusal of a default deployable fraction | **ARC 032** — CLAUDE.md makes §12A the semantic authority for tunables (names, defaults, cross-knob boot validation) and per-module JSON the physical layout, with a boot-loaded restart-only lifecycle. A ceiling with a default is a ceiling nobody chose, so refusing one is the right INTERIM behaviour, but a ceiling every caller chooses separately is the same defect distributed. Note that `nixrisk.picture.PictureMirror` already takes the same undeclared `max_age_s` (ARC 028), so this row is one knob owed for two readers and is coupled to D3.123. Discharge = a named §12A knob with a default the spec chooses, a `risks/` entry carrying it, and boot validation against §6.4's stale-cache rule | verify |
<!-- ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent B) — the sizing pathway. Appended at the end so integration conflicts are trivial. -->
| D3.126 | `scripts/nixalloc/sizing.py` — **the frozen spec gives instrument selection TWO different positions in the pipeline and the module had to pick one** | **opened ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent B) by the author of the choice, in the same motion as the choice, open.** `nics_risk_subsystem_spec_v1.3.md` §3:132 writes `size = min(risk_contracts, margin_contracts, symbol_cap) -> instrument selection -> FCFS -> correlation cap`, putting selection AFTER the `min`. §7:488-493 writes the selection rule as a function of the RISK-ideal alone (*"compute ideal size in micro units ... if risk-ideal quantizes acceptably to fulls ... fulls only; otherwise micros only"*), which puts it BEFORE the other two terms. §7's order is what shipped, and the reason is a measurement rather than a preference: `margin_contracts` divides by the live per-symbol margin and `symbol_cap` is a per-instrument ceiling, so neither term is DEFINED until the instrument is known (ES margin is not MES margin), and under §3's literal order a risk-ideal of 0.6 fulls floors to `min(...) = 0` and denies before micros are considered at all — which defeats the granularity micros exist for. The two orders agree on every input where the full contract is selected, so nothing observable separates them there. **DISCHARGED ARC 031 (Phase 5) by ARCHITECT RULING, recorded as `SPEC-A8`.** §7 GOVERNS: selection is PRIOR to `min(risk, margin, symbol_cap)` and a function of the risk-ideal ALONE, because `margin_contracts` divides by live per-symbol margin and `symbol_cap` is per-instrument — neither term is DEFINED until the instrument is known; §3:132's order denies valid trades before micros are considered and is the incoherent one. §3:132 is amended to POINT at §7's pipeline rather than restate it (one source, core directive 3), and the frozen document is NOT edited. **The ruling ratifies what shipped, so no code moved** — `scripts/nixalloc/sizing.py` already implements §7's order, `check_allocator_pathway` and the Stage-1 suites are unchanged and stay green, and no re-measure is owed because nothing changed. The row is kept in the count under this ledger's convention: the finding was real, it was opened by the author of the choice in the same motion as the choice, and that is the record worth keeping | ARC 032 | allocator |
| D3.127 | `scripts/nixalloc/sizing.py` — **`SYMBOL_CAP`'s UNITS are undefined for a micro leg, and the module had to choose a normalisation** | **opened ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent B), open.** §12A:807 gives `SYMBOL_CAP` as "per-symbol max contracts" and `risks/allocator.config.json` keys it by the five logical symbols (ES, NQ, CL, GC, ZN) — there is no MES/MNQ key and no statement of whether the integer counts full contracts or the selected instrument's own units. Capping micros at the same integer would make the cap ten times tighter for a micros trade than for a fulls trade at identical dollar exposure; the module therefore multiplies the cap by the instrument's units-per-full, which makes it an EXPOSURE ceiling and is derived from the only normalisation the spec itself states (§7:502, *"Micros count at 1/10 weight"*). **That derivation is a reading, not a quotation, and it changes how much risk a micros proposal may carry — so it is recorded rather than left in a docstring.** Discharge = the §12A knob's semantics stated explicitly, or the cap re-expressed in dollar risk | ARC 032 | allocator |
| D3.128 | `scripts/nixalloc/sizing.py` `InstrumentSpec` — **the sizing pathway CANNOT RUN on this box: `tick_value` has no source, no loader and no provisioning path** | **opened ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent B), MEASURED — the tree was searched and no `tick_value` exists outside test fixtures and prose.** §12A declares no such knob, so `risks/` is correctly not its home (adding one would have invented a tunable and reddened `checks/check_risks_data_only.py` ARM 2's one-home rule), and the strategy contract's section 7.2 forbids hardcoding it and delivers it on the registration ACK — a path that does not exist in this tree. The module therefore takes `Mapping[str, InstrumentSpec]` by INJECTION and refuses (`NOT_TRADABLE`) for any symbol with no spec, which is honest and also means **every green in `checks/check_allocator_sizing.py` is over specs the gate itself constructed.** Discharge = the registration/ACK path, or a contract-spec store, whichever the strategy-seam arc lands | ARC 032 | allocator |
| D3.129 | `scripts/nixalloc/sizing.py` — **the §7 correlation-bucket cap is DECLARED and NOT APPLIED; the sizing pathway ships with the cap unwired** | **opened ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent B) by the author, in the same motion, open.** `BucketCapPort` is a required constructor argument with no default so that `None` has to be passed out loud, and every `SizingRationale.note` produced with `None` says *"§7 correlation-bucket cap NOT APPLIED"*. `checks/check_allocator_sizing.py` drives it as `None` and asserts that sentence is present, so a green there provably does NOT imply cap coverage. The implementation is `scripts/nixalloc/caps.py`, a different owner in the same arc; **what is owed here is the WIRING and a gate over it** — until that lands, a proposal's dollar risk is bounded only by the portfolio-wide layers (§6.5), never by within-bucket concentration | ARC 032 | allocator |
| D3.130 | `checks/check_allocator_sizing.py` ARM 6 — **the one-versioned-row identity is proven WITHIN ONE PROCESS, and for per-symbol margin it is proven one step weaker still** | **opened ARC 031 (Stage 1, sub-agent B) by the gate's author, stated rather than left to be found.** §6.4 promises the Allocator and the Limiter's gate read *"the same versioned row ... identical bytes by construction"*. ARM 6 hands ONE picture object to the shipped sizer and to the real `nixrisk.gate.GatePass`, records every field each reads, and requires identical values on every shared field plus the version stamp — which is real evidence, and is evidence about ONE PROCESS. The wire between two processes is `scripts/nixrisk/picture.py`'s codec, whose subject belongs to `check_picture_atomicity`, and nothing yet drives the two ends together. **Second, narrower gap, measured:** the gate's `AggregateMarginCapRule` and `DeployableCeilingRule` divide by `order.margin_per_contract`, never `picture.margin_per_contract`, so for that ONE figure the identity is carried by the Allocator's COPY. The strongest provable statement is copy-equality at the sized version, and that is what the gate asserts — the gap is named in the gate's own docstring and asserted to still exist, so the claim self-invalidates if `gate.py` starts reading the picture instead | ARC 032 | allocator |
| D3.131 | `risks/allocator_caps.config.json`'s boot-rule correspondence is checked in ONE DIRECTION ONLY | ARC 031 Stage 1 C, MEASURED by reading `checks/check_risks_data_only.py`'s own two halves and running it against the landed file: `_arm4_boot_rules` proves every id the JSON DECLARES is implemented by the module it names (`scripts/nixalloc/caps.py`'s `BOOT_RULE_IDS`), but the reverse half — `_validated_configs`, which asserts every rule the validator APPLIES is written down in the JSON — iterates `risk_config.OWNED_MODULES`, and `allocator_caps` is not one (its loader is `nixalloc.caps.load_cap_config`, not `risk_config`). Gate is GREEN on the landed file and the one-way hole is real: a rule `caps.py` starts enforcing without declaring it in the JSON is invisible to the gate | ARC 032 — discharge is either (a) teach `check_risks_data_only` to read a validator's `BOOT_RULE_IDS` and require the JSON to declare every one of them regardless of who owns the module (the general fix, and it also covers `risks/broker_order.config.json`'s separate loader), or (b) bring `allocator_caps` under `risk_config.OWNED_MODULES`, which trades this hole for a coupling the module deliberately avoided. `scripts/nixalloc/caps.py::_validate` closes the *vacuity* half itself — it raises when the rules it evaluated do not number `len(BOOT_RULE_IDS)` — so a validator that silently ran fewer rules than it declares is already caught; what is not caught is one that runs MORE | verify |
| D3.132 | §6.6:459's *"the **Allocator reads** it to weight sizing"* is NOT implemented — `scripts/nixalloc/contention.py` produces an ORDERING and a NEUTRAL weight, never a score-derived sizing weight | ARC 031 Stage 1 C, MEASURED and self-declared: `ContentionRanking.weights` is `NEUTRAL_WEIGHT` (1.0) for every contender under BOTH `FCFS` and `PERFORMANCE_WEIGHTED`, `scripts/tests/test_allocator_caps.py::test_the_weights_are_NEUTRAL_under_both_policies_and_say_so` asserts exactly that, and the ranking's own `reason` string says the transform is deferred. `checks/check_allocator_caps.py`'s WHAT-THIS-GATE-CANNOT-PROVE block names it too, so a green there cannot be read as coverage of it | ARC 032 — but the discharge is R5's, not this ledger's to force. §6.6 fixes NO transform from a realized-P&L EMA to a sizing weight, and §6.6:461-463 makes "defining productive" the allocation judgment that stays out of the consumer, so inventing one here would have been the authority violation the seam was landed to prevent. Deliberately named rather than built: the transform lands with the Scoring process (R5, §12B) that publishes the score it would consume, and until then a neutral weight is the honest value and the reason string is the honest disclosure | verify |
| D3.133 | There is NO configured staleness threshold for the §6.6 ranking table — §12A defines one for margin, calendar, price and balance, and none for the score | ARC 031 Stage 1 C, MEASURED against the frozen spec: §12A's *Feeds / staleness* block names `MARGIN_STALE_MS`, `CALENDAR_STALE_MS`, `PRICE_STALE_MS`, `BALANCE_STALE_MS` and `CLOCK_SKEW_MAX_MS`, and nothing for the ranking table, while §6.6:465 makes "its table is stale" a locked fallback trigger. So the trigger exists in the rule and its threshold exists nowhere | ARC 032 — `nixalloc.contention.rank` does not invent one: `RankingTablePort.available()` is treated as the sole authority on absent-or-stale, and the optional `max_age_s`/`now` pair is applied only when a CALLER supplies both (driven by `test_a_STALE_ROW_is_treated_as_absent_when_an_age_is_supplied`). Discharge = the arc that builds the Scoring process (R5) or the ranking-table transport decides the threshold and gives it a §12A-shaped home; a knob minted here would be a semantic decision made in the physical layer, which `risks/` may not become. Note the failure mode is BENIGN by construction — an unthresholded table degrades toward being trusted too long, and §6.6:467 already forbids the opposite (halting), so the safe default is the fallback and the fallback is what runs today | verify |
| D3.134 | `risks/allocator_caps.config.json`'s `tick_value_usd` is an INSTRUMENT-CONSTANT table living in the risk-tunables directory, with no venue source and no gate that can verify it | ARC 031 Stage 1 C, MEASURED: §7:476 and §7:501 both require `tick_value` by name, `grep` over `risks/`, `scripts/` and `checks/` finds no other physical home for it in the tree, and `docs/directory_structure.md`'s standing rule for `risks/` is *"data-role expression of the risk spec + §12A knobs"* — a tick value is neither. It is landed there with an explicit `not a §12A knob` derivation because the alternative was a literal typed into `caps.py`, which is strictly worse. Until that cache exists there is nothing to derive from and no instrument can do better than assert the table covers §7:498's bucketed symbols, which `caps.tick_values_cover_every_bucketed_symbol` already does. | D3.135 | `checks/check_allocator_caps.py` proves the §7 cap RULE and the §6.6 FALLBACK; it proves nothing about the PATHWAY, because no Allocator process and no sizing pass calls either one | ARC 031 Stage 1 C, MEASURED by construction — every arm of the gate calls `nixalloc.caps.admit` / `nixalloc.contention.rank` DIRECTLY, and `grep` over the tree finds no caller of either outside this arc's own gate and tests. §7:478's `size = min(risk_contracts, margin_contracts, symbol_cap) -> variant selection -> contention -> correlation cap` makes the cap the LAST term of a chain whose earlier terms are sub-agent B's (`scripts/nixalloc/sizing.py`); nothing mechanically proves that chain calls this term, and a sizing pathway that silently skipped the cap would leave this gate green | ARC 032 — discharge = an instrument over the ASSEMBLED pathway: a GO driven end-to-end through the sizing pass, asserting the emitted `Proposal.rationale.binding` is `BUCKET_CAP` on a case where only the cap can bind, and reddening when the cap term is removed from the chain. That instrument cannot be built inside a file-disjoint sub-agent split — it spans `sizing.py` (B), `mirror.py` (A) and `caps.py` (C) — so it belongs to the integrator or to the next arc, and it is named here rather than implied by a green over three separately-correct parts | verify |

<!-- ARC 031 (Stage 2, integration) — what composing the three Stage-1 pieces FOUND. -->
| D3.136 | **§7's correlation-bucket cap CANNOT BE COMPUTED from the published financial picture, and all three Stage-1 gates were green while it could not run** | ARC 031 (Stage 2, integration), MEASURED by composing sub-agents A, B and C for the first time — each built against the frozen seam with its own doubles, on purpose, so none of them could see this | **DISCHARGED ARC 032 (Stage 2), and the discharge is a BEFORE/AFTER rather than an after.** `PositionRow.stop_distance` rides the published snapshot (`SEAM_REV 1.1.0`, `WIRE_SCHEMA 2`, `SPEC-A9`), `PublishedExposures` prices a held position from the same versioned row that carries `balance`, and the out-of-band stop table this ledger's own text describes was DELETED rather than kept as a fallback — a retained side table is a second unversioned input a gate can manufacture, which is the shape that produced this row. **MEASURED on ONE scenario, two code paths, the BEFORE half being the actual pre-widening `wiring.py`/`caps.py`/`sizing.py` and both seams checked out of git and executed** (found by `-S POSITION_ROW_FIELDS`, never a hard-coded sha): two held same-bucket positions (ES 2 @ 20t, NQ 3 @ 20t), a 1.5% ceiling on a $100,000 balance, one third ES proposal. BEFORE: **36 contracts admitted**, binding `risk`, `bucket_used` **$0.00**, cap INCOMPLETE. AFTER: **22 contracts**, binding `bucket_cap`, `bucket_used` **$880.00** — which is $550 (ES) + $330 (NQ), neither position alone and not the larger of them, so the figure cannot be produced by a max-shaped or single-position cap. **Fourteen extra contracts, 63% more, admitted on identical inputs.** The loader carries its own non-vacuity assertion (the pre-widening `PositionRow` must NOT have `stop_distance`) and that assertion FIRED on the first draft, which restored `sys.modules` per module and so let the 'before' half silently be the 'after' half. **TWO successor rows, both in the SAME admitting direction and neither closed by this discharge: D3.142** (the published `symbol` has no pinned vocabulary, so a contract-spelled row never reaches its bucket at all — priced at zero by OMISSION) and **D3.150** (nothing in production ever CHOOSES a `stop_distance`, so §7's cap is fed a fixture literal). The original disposition, kept because the reason a gap existed outlives the gap: unassigned — architect ruling. §7:501 fixes the exposure unit as `(stop_ticks + slippage_pad) × tick_value × contracts`, so applying the cap to the positions ALREADY in a bucket needs each held position's STOP DISTANCE. The published `PositionRow` (`scripts/nixrisk/seam.py`, frozen) carries `trade_id`, `symbol`, `strategy_id`, `size`, `margin`, `state` — **no stop distance at all**. The distance lives in the Limiter's stop book as `StopState.initial_distance_ticks`, keyed by `client_order_id`, and the stop book is not published. Both possible input paths are closed today: (a) reading the stop book as a SECOND table is the cross-table skew §6.4 refuses in the same breath as it fixes one snapshot, and nothing publishes it anyway; (b) putting the distance on the published row is a change to the one snapshot §3 makes atomic — a `SEAM_REV` bump and an architect ruling, not an implementation detail. **Why no Stage-1 gate could see it:** sub-agent C drove `caps.admit` with `Exposure` rows it constructed itself, and sub-agent B drove `BucketCapPort` with `None` and asserted the not-applied sentence. The argument between them was never made until `scripts/nixalloc/wiring.py` made it. **The direction matters and is measured, not asserted:** an unpriced position valued at zero makes the bucket look EMPTIER than it is, and an emptier bucket ADMITS more — `checks/check_allocator_pathway.py` ARM 3 drives both directions and reddens if the admitting direction ever reverses. Until this is discharged, `PublishedExposures` prices nothing on a real snapshot, `PathwayReport.cap_incomplete` is True, and every §16 U5 rationale says so. **RULED ARC 031 (Phase 5) — OPTION A, and the ruling is RECORDED here rather than executed: R3-B builds it.** Add `stop_distance` to the published `PositionRow`. The deciding argument is that the cap is a **safety input currently failing OPEN** — an unpriced position reads as zero risk, the bucket looks emptier than it is, and an emptier bucket ADMITS more — so the gap is not merely incomplete, it is incomplete in the permissive direction. The skew-free fix is `stop_distance` riding the **same versioned snapshot** as `balance` and `positions`: one more field under ONE writer and ONE version stamp (§6.4b's principle). Path (a), the stop-book read, is REFUSED by name — that IS the cross-table skew §6.4 forbids. This is a **`SEAM_REV` bump**: the Limiter (sole writer) adds the field, every mirror consumer widens, `nixalloc.seam.MIRRORED_FIELDS` gains it, `SEAM_REV` 1.0.0 -> **1.1.0**, and R3-B **re-proves the one-versioned-row identity across the WIDER schema** rather than assuming the widening preserved it. **NOT IMPLEMENTED THIS TURN, deliberately** — the planned target is recorded at the literal in `scripts/nixalloc/seam.py` and the ruling at the finding in `scripts/nixalloc/wiring.py`, so the next arc reads the decision where the decision binds. Row stays OPEN: a recorded ruling is not a landed mechanism | ARC 032 (R3-B, opening item) | allocator |
| D3.137 | **`BucketCapPort`'s original signature could not express §7's own inputs, and the first adapter built behind it reported "cap NOT APPLIED" for every equities proposal while every gate stayed green** | ARC 031 (Stage 2, integration), MEASURED — the integration test caught it on the normal case, not on an edge | **discharged ARC 031 (Stage 2)** — the port passed `(bucket, contracts, risk_per_contract, picture)`. §7:501 prices exposure from the LOGICAL symbol (which resolves the bucket, the per-symbol pad and the tick value), the STOP DISTANCE, and whether instrument selection chose the micro leg (§7:502 counts micros at 1/10). None of the three is recoverable from those four arguments: `equities` holds TWO symbols, so `bucket` alone does not name the proposal's symbol. The first adapter tried to recover it from the bucket plus the margin table and was ambiguous on exactly the normal case — a snapshot pricing both ES and MES and NQ and MNQ — so it returned "cap NOT APPLIED" and the proposal went through UNCAPPED. So the declaration is true exactly when `verify.py` runs under a venv and FALSE when it runs under the system interpreter — **which `scripts/verify.py`'s own module docstring names as a supported invocation**: *"Stdlib only (§9.1) so it runs under system python3 before .venv exists."* The comment above the declaration says basename matching covers the interpreter *"regardless of which venv's `python` answers on this box"*; that is true across venvs and false for the one interpreter that is not in a venv, and the comment is the part that misleads. **BOTH HALVES RUN, same tree, same commit, only the interpreter changed:** under `/usr/bin/python3` `check_observed_resource_claims` is **FAIL** naming the claim verbatim (*'was OBSERVED using subprocess:/usr/bin/python3 and its declaration ... does not account for it — a false declaration (D2.27)'*); under `.venv/bin/python` it is **CANNOT_MEASURE** (the standing tap-session ECONNREFUSED, §17) with the `check_extract_sources` finding ABSENT. The verdict is correct in both directions and §17's rule decides which wins: a positively-observed undeclared claim outranks masking, so FAIL is the honest verdict and not an artifact of how the run was launched. **Recorded rather than fixed, deliberately** (doctrine A.4, and CLAUDE.md directive 7): the one-token repair is adding `'subprocess:python3'` to the tuple, but `RESOURCES` is read statically by `verify.py` and derived into the block plan's disjointness, so widening a declaration inside a phase whose whole instruction was *record the rulings* is scope this phase does not have. **What this row is really about is bigger than one token:** every `RESOURCES` declaration in the tree is verified against ONE interpreter per run, so any other declaration with the same latent split is currently unmeasured in one of the two documented launch modes. Exactly TWO production constructions of `PositionRow` exist: `nixrisk/picture.py:_decode_row`, which READS the field off the wire, and `nixrisk/flatten.py:_confirmed_rows`, which CARRIES it from the mirror's row and whose own comment says inventing one there would publish a §7:501 exposure no sizing pass ever computed. **No third site exists, so no code path assigns a distance at origin.** ARC 032 proved the field TRAVELS — seam, codec, real wire, mirror, and across a real process boundary — and proved nothing whatever about it being RIGHT; those are two different claims and only the first is banked. **The direction is still the permissive one:** §7:501 prices bucket exposure from the distance, so a row published with a placeholder feeds §7's cap a number no sizing pass computed, and a wrong-but-present value is not obviously safer than the absent one it replaced. Discharge = the origin write (the Limiter's fill path setting `stop_distance` from the accepted `Proposal`/`StopState`), PLUS a gate that reddens when a published row's distance disagrees with the stop book's `initial_distance_ticks` for the same trade. **Recorded now rather than after the fact, because the widening makes this the ONLY remaining hole between §7's cap and a real number** | ARC 033 | allocator |

The in-memory races each carry a live plant and a floor; the wire does not, so its stop-axis power comes entirely from a can-fail control (`test_a_SINK_THAT_STRIPS_STOP_DISTANCE_reddens_the_WIRE_and_the_LATE_arms`) that runs in pytest and not in the gate. A run of the SHIPPED gate on a box where the suite is not run reports `0 torn` on the wire with no in-run evidence that a torn wire would have been seen — **so the two greens in one evidence string are not the same kind of green.** This is the asymmetry `MIN_STOP_TEARS` closed for the races, one transport out. | D3.152 | **A declared `RESOURCES` token that names nothing the check touches is UNFALSIFIABLE, and 24 of 50 checks carry at least one** | ARC 032 (Stage 1 B), MEASURED as a by-product of D3.140's both-interpreters sweep — every declared token run through the shipped `covers()` against the union of what that check was OBSERVED claiming under BOTH documented interpreters, 100 observations | ARC 033. `check_observed_resource_claims` owns exactly one direction — *"is what it said TRUE"* — which catches UNDER-declaration. The other direction is silent by construction: a token covering no observed claim is never a finding, so `RESOURCES` can name anything at all and this gate will not notice. Most of the 24 are honestly unfalsifiable — `interpreter:sys.modules`, `interpreter:sys.path`, `zmq-ipc`, `cpu-affinity`, `ptrace`, `shm`, `git-hooks`, `pre-commit-store`, `state/node_identity.json`, `network:pypi` name classes the PEP 578 observer does not watch, and a gate reddening on them would report its own blind spot as the subject's defect. **Two are INSIDE the vocabulary and were simply never exercised:** `check_runtime_gate` declares `subprocess:python` and was observed spawning only `subprocess:git` under both interpreters; `check_python_deps` declares `network:pypi` and made no non-loopback socket claim under either. Those two are indistinguishable, with this instrument, from a code path this box does not take — the residual the gate already records one level up. **NOT fixed here:** deleting a token because one run did not exercise it is the observer rewriting declarations to match what it just saw, which the gate's own `NON_CORRECTABLE_REASON` refuses by name. | D3.153 | **`documented_interpreters` substitutes the OBSERVER's own venv when `nix_home` has none, so a wrong-tree venv would be swept under the label of the right one** | ARC 032 (Stage 1 B), MEASURED — the fallback fires on every synthetic population in the gate's own suite and is the reason its existing plant/control tests still measure anything | ARC 033. The venv launch mode is `<nix_home>/.venv/bin/python`. Against a synthetic tree there is no venv under `nix_home`, and returning a path that does not exist would make `check_observed_resource_claims` report "one launch mode unavailable" on every one of its own fixtures — **a gate blind on its fixtures measures nothing on them.** So when `nix_home` has no venv and the observer is itself running from one, THAT venv is used. **The residual, stated rather than implied:** `verify.py` pointed at a tree whose venv is genuinely missing, launched from some other tree's venv, sweeps the wrong venv under the label `venv`. It is not silent — the evidence line prints the requested path and what that interpreter reported itself to be — but it is not REFUSED either, and §17's rule is that a property proven while its subject is unavailable is not proven. Discharge = decide whether an absent `<nix_home>/.venv` is CANNOT_MEASURE (honest, and turns every synthetic-population test in that suite unmeasurable), or whether the fixture case is distinguishable from the production case by something better than *"a venv exists somewhere"* | ARC 033 | verify |

| D3.177 | **The `trade_id` <-> `client_order_id` relationship is UNDEFINED in the frozen spec, and ARC 033/0.2 shipped a DEFAULT BINDING in the absence of a ruling** | ARC 033 (0.2), MEASURED by enumeration: §3:159 keys the position table by `trade_id` and §4 mints it at open, while `StopState`, `ProposedOrder`, `Reservation` and §4's `(order_id, exec_id)` dedup tuple are ALL keyed by `client_order_id`. No code and no spec sentence joins them, so the arc brief's own success criterion — *"the published stop_distance for the same trade"* — was **not expressible** | unassigned — architect. This is the D3.137 shape (a seam that cannot express its own inputs) one module over. **The decision was made a SURFACE rather than an equality:** `seam.TradeOrigin` / `TradeOriginPort` declare it, `positions.EntryOrderOrigins` implements it, and the minting policy is an INJECTED callable whose default (`identity_trade_id` — a trade IS the entry order that opened it) is documented and overridable. That matters because under the default the two ids are EQUAL, so a writer that hard-coded the equality emits byte-identical rows and **no drive over the default can see it**; `check_origin_write` ARM JOIN re-drives the whole population under a NON-IDENTITY mint, which is the only way the defect is visible. Sound only inside the declared scope (intraday-only §6.1b, one strategy per instrument, one in-flight action per strategy §4); a scaling order, a position spanning a session, or a venue-side order-id re-issue each break the bijection.
